\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-36.99486pt}{39.00795pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

This achieves to prove that bicompacts of
{\bf ps}[2,\mathcal{B}]
are exactly arrows between bicompacts.

Finally we have to prove that any arrow is a bifiltered bicolimit of bicompact arrows. For any
f
we have both that
{\rm dom}(f)={\textup{bicolim}}\,\mathcal{B}_{\omega}\downarrow{\rm dom}(f)
and
{\rm cod}(f)={\textup{bicolim}}\,\mathcal{B}_{\omega}\downarrow{\rm cod}(f)
, and moreover
f
is induced as
f=\langle fa\rangle_{\mathcal{B}_{\omega}\downarrow{\rm dom}(f)}
. But now, as
\mathcal{B}_{\omega}\downarrow{\rm cod}(f)
for each
a:K\rightarrow{\rm dom}(f)
we can pick a lift

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm dom}\,f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm cod}\,f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta\atop\simeq}

and
f
is exhibited as the bicolimit of the subcategory of
{\bf ps}[2,\mathcal{B}_{\omega}]\downarrow f
consisting of all those lifts
(a,b,\beta)
for
a:K\rightarrow{\rm dom}(f)
and
(b,\beta)
a lift of
fa
. Now checking that this subcategory is bifiltered is a straitforward utilisation of the bifilteredness of the canonical cones of the domain and codomain.
∎

4
2-Categories of flat pseudofunctors

We deduced several properties of the bi-accessible and bipresentable 2-categories from analysing their binerve pseudofunctors, which allowed to see them as 2-categories of
{\bf Cat}
-valued pseudofunctors. Here we describe the precise class of pseudofunctors obtained through this process, the analog of the ordinary
flat
functors. They were defined in
[
DDS18
]
, from which we give the following definitions and elementary property. As in the 1-dimensional case, those are pseudofunctors that virtually preserves finitely weighted bilimits whenever they exist (which amounts to testing real preservation at the level of the left biKan extension); it was also remarked that this amounted to requiring their category of elements to be
\sigma
-cofiltered relatively to their opcartesian morphisms. We give a further simplification of this latter property into a condition of bifilteredness thanks to our key observation, which harmonizes this result with our definitions of bi-accessibility. We then prove the 2-categories of flat pseudofunctors to be themselves bi-accessible and bipresentable whenever their domain 2-category have finite weighted bilimits.

4.1
Extension of flat pseudofunctors

Definition 4.1.1

(Bilex
2
-categories)
.

A
bilex
2-category is a 2-category with all finite weighted bilimits as defined in
Definition

1.1.5
, and a pseudofunctor is said to be
bilex
if it preserves them (up to equivalence). For a small bilex 2-category
\mathcal{C}
and a 2-category
\mathcal{D}
we write
{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}]
the 2-category of bilex pseudofunctors from
\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{D}
.

This very brief subsection sets the stage for the later discussions, we will study extensions of pseudofunctors along the Yoneda 2-embedding into the 2-category of pseudofunctors into
{\bf Cat}
(which is biequivalent to the 2-category of strict 2-functors and pseudonatural transformations)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathcal{C}^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}]_{p}\simeq{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C}^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}]}}

We will use pseudofunctors into
{\bf Cat}
to keep the correct level of strictness; again, for
{\bf Cat}
is a strict 2-category, pseudofunctors always form a strict 2-category, as stated for instance at
[
JY21
]
[Corollary 4.4.13].

Definition 4.1.2

(Flat pseudofunctor
[
DDS18
, Def. 4.1.11]
)
.

A pseudofunctor
F:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
is
flat
if its left biKan extension
{\textup{biLan}}_{\text{\char 136\relax}}F
is bilex. In particular, a flat pseudofunctor preserves any finitely weighted bilimit already existing in
\mathcal{C}
.

Proposition 4.1.3

(
[
DDS18
, Prop 4.1.14]
)
.

Corepresentable 2-functors
\text{\char 232\relax}_{C}:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
are flat 2-functors. Their biKan extension can be chosen to be the evaluation functor at the corresponding object
C
.

In
[
DDS18
]
, we find this crucial theorem which provides the 2-dimensional Diaconescu theorem of extension of flat (pseudo)functors. Our formulation puts together two results in
[
DDS18
]
, which account on the possible levels of strinctness of the result.

Theorem 4.1.4

(
[
DDS18
, Prop 4.2.7 and A.6]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small 2-category. Then for a 2-functor (resp. pseudofunctor)
F:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
we have the following equivalences

-

F
is flat, that is,
{\textup{biLan}}_{\text{\char 136\relax}}F
is bilex

-

(\int F)^{{\rm op}}
is
\sigma
-filtered relatively to the class of opcartesian arrows

-

F
is a
\sigma
-filtered pseudocolimit (resp. bicolimit) of representables in
[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]_{p}
(resp. in
{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
).

But in the regard of
Lemma

2.2.6
, it appears that one can complete this theorem with a last item simplifying the
\sigma
-filtered decomposition into a bifiltered one:

Lemma 4.1.5

(Flatness as a bifilteredness condition)
.

A pseudofunctor
F:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
is flat if and only if it decomposes as a bifiltered bicolimit of representables.

Proof.

In
Theorem

4.1.4
, the last item is obtained by combining the general fact that any pseudofunctor
F
is the
\sigma
-bicolimit of the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\displaystyle\int F)^{{\rm op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}^{{\rm op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{F}}

for the class of opposites of opcartesian morphisms. Now
F
is flat if and only if
((\int F)^{{\rm op}},\textrm{op}{\bf Cart}^{{\rm op}})
is a
\sigma
-filtered pair, which amounts by
Lemma

2.2.6
to saying that the full on 0-cells and 2-cells subcategory
\iota_{F}:\textrm{op}{\bf Cart}^{{\rm op}}\hookrightarrow(\int F)^{{\rm op}}
is bifiltered and
\sigma
-cofinal in
(\int F)^{{\rm op}}
relatively to itself. But then by
Proposition

2.2.5
, those two observations yield altogether that we have a bifiltered bicolimit

F\simeq\underset{\textrm{op}{\bf Cart}^{{\rm op}}}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\text{\char 232\relax}\pi_{F}\iota_{F}

over the restriction of its 2-category of elements to opcartesian morphisms.
∎

Remark 4.1.6
.

This result just is dual to our observations that the pseudoslice restricted to bicompact objects provides a convenient notion of canonical diagram in a bi-accessible category. We are precisely going to see why in the next section.

Now it appears that we already encountered flat pseudofunctors when examining bi-accessible and bipresentable 2-categories: their binerve pseudofunctor identified them with 2-categories of flat pseudofunctors over their generator of bicompact object:

Theorem 4.1.7

(Canonical representation of bi-accessible
2
-categories)
.

For a finitely bi-accessible 2-category
\mathcal{B}
, we have a biequivalence

\mathcal{B}\simeq{\bf Flat}_{\bf ps}[(\mathcal{B}_{\omega})^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}]

Proof.

By
Remark

3.2.12
,
\mathcal{B}
is equivalent to a full on 1-cells and 2-cells sub-2-category of
{\bf ps}[(\mathcal{B}_{\omega})^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}]
which is moreover closed under bifiltered bicolimits by
Lemma

3.2.13
. On the other hand, we know from
Lemma

3.2.5
that for each
B
the canonical pseudococone
\mathcal{B}_{\omega}\downarrow B
is bifiltered, or equivalently that the oplax cocone
\mathcal{B}_{\omega}\Downarrow B
is
\sigma
-filtered relatively to
{\bf Cart}_{B}
: but we saw at
3.2.3
that the oplax cocone is exactly the category of elements of the image
\nu_{B}
of
B
along the binerve; this exactly means that
\nu_{B}
is flat, so that
\nu
factorizes through the inclusion
{\bf Flat}_{\bf ps}[(\mathcal{B}_{\omega})^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}]\hookrightarrow{\bf ps}[(\mathcal{B}_{\omega})^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}]
, exhibiting
\mathcal{B}
as consisting of flat pseudofunctors.

For the converse, observe that representable 2-functors of the form
\text{\char 136\relax}_{K}:(\mathcal{B}_{\omega})^{{\rm op}}\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
are in particular equivalent to the image of the underlying object along the binerve, that is
\text{\char 136\relax}_{K}\simeq\nu_{K}
, so they are at the same time flat and in the range of
\nu
. But by
Theorem

4.1.4
any flat pseudofunctor is a
\sigma
-filtered bicolimit of representables; for
\nu
preserves
\sigma
-filtered bicolimits, this forces any flat functor to be equivalent to a functor of the form
\nu_{B}
for
B
in
\mathcal{B}
.
∎

In the presence of finite weighted bilimits, flatness simplifies as follows:

Proposition 4.1.8

(
[
DDS18
]
Proposition 4.2.9)
.

If
\mathcal{C}
has finite weighted bilimits, then flat pseudofunctors
\mathcal{C}\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
are exactly the bilex ones, that is that we have a biequivalence

{\bf Flat}_{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]\simeq{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]

In particular, in the case of a finitely bipresentable 2-category, where the generator of bicompact object is closed under finite weighted bicolimits,
Theorem

4.1.7
reduces to the following:

Theorem 4.1.9

(Representation theorem for finitely bipresentable
2
-categories)
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a finitely bipresentable
2
-category. Then the binerve pseudofunctor induces a biequivalence of
2
-categories with bilex pseudofunctors

\mathcal{B}\simeq\textup{{\bf biLex}}[(\mathcal{B}_{\omega})^{\rm op},{\bf Cat}]

4.2
2-category of flat pseudofunctors are finitely bi-accessibles

In this subsection, we want to exhibit the relation between a small bilex category and the bicompact objects of the associated category of pseudofunctors. The results of this part are actually quite similar to the strategy in the 1-categorical context:

4.2.2

we first prove that 2-categories of flat pseudofunctors have
\sigma
-filtered colimits which are computed in the 2-categories of pseudofunctors,

4.2.4

then we prove that bicorepresentable are always bicompact in 2-categories of flat pseudofunctors,

4.2.5

finally, we prove that bicompact objects are pseudoretracts of birepresentables in
{\bf Flat}_{{\bf ps}}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
.

First of all, observe that for
\mathcal{C}
a small 2-category, birepresentables are in
{\bf Flat}_{{\bf ps}}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
. This is an immediate consequence of preservation of any bilimit by corepresentables. Hence the Yoneda embedding
factorizes through the 2-category of flat pseudofunctors.

Lemma 4.2.1
.

For
\mathcal{C}
a small 2-category, birepresentables are
\sigma
-tiny in
{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
.

Proof.

Let
F:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
be a flat pseudofunctor equipped with a pseudonatural equivalence
F\simeq\text{\char 232\relax}_{A}

and
G:I\rightarrow{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
with
I
a small 2-category equipped with a class
\Sigma
, then we have a sequence of equivalences

\displaystyle{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]\big[F,{\Sigma{\textup{bicolim}}}\,G\big]

\displaystyle\simeq{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]\big[\text{\char 232\relax}_{A},{\Sigma{\textup{bicolim}}}\,G\big]

\displaystyle\simeq({\Sigma{\textup{bicolim}}}\,G)(A)

\displaystyle\simeq\underset{i\in I}{\Sigma{\textup{bicolim}}}\,G(i)(A)

\displaystyle\simeq\underset{i\in I}{\Sigma{\textup{bicolim}}}\,{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]\big[\text{\char 232\relax}_{A},G(i)\big]

\displaystyle\simeq\underset{i\in I}{\Sigma{\textup{bicolim}}}\,{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]\big[F,G(i)\big]

where the third equivalence comes from the fact that
\sigma
-bicolimits are pointwise in pseudofunctors categories.
∎

Proposition 4.2.2
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a small 2-category, then the 2-category
{\bf Flat}_{{\bf ps}}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
has
\sigma
-filtered bicolimits. Moreover they are created by the pseudo-fully faithful inclusion

{\bf Flat}_{{\bf ps}}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{C}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]

Proof.

See
[
Des20
]
[2.3 and 2.3.5].
∎

Corollary 4.2.3
.

As a consequence,
{\bf Flat}_{{\bf ps}}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
also has bifiltered bicolimits created by the inclusion.

Corollary 4.2.4
.

For any small 2-category
\mathcal{C}
, bicorepresentables are bicompact in
{\bf Flat}_{{\bf ps}}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]

Proof.

From
Lemma

4.2.1
, corepresentables are
\sigma
-tiny in
{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
, where they are hence bicompact; and as
{\bf Flat}_{{\bf ps}}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
is closed in
{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
under bifiltered bicolimits, we are done.
∎

In the general case, there are more bicompacts than bicorepresentables in 2-categories of flat functors; we would like to characterize those bicompact objects.

Proposition 4.2.5
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an arbitrary 2-category. Then any bicompact object in
{\bf Flat}_{{\bf ps}}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
is a pseudoretract of a bicorepresentable.

Proof.

Let
K:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
be a flat pseudofunctor which is bicompact in
{\bf Flat}_{{\bf ps}}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
and
r_{K},s_{K},\alpha,\beta,\theta
as above.
K
being flat, it decomposes as a bifiltered bicolimit by
Lemma

4.1.5
and there exists a pseudonatural equivalence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I_{K}}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,\text{\char 232\relax}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I_{K}}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,\text{\char 232\relax}_{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta\atop\simeq}

But hypothesis that
K
is bicompact, there is for some
(A,a)
an invertible 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I_{K}}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,\text{\char 232\relax}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{\char 232\relax}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{(A,a)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi\atop\simeq}

we can paste with
\alpha
to exhibit
x
as a pseudosection of
s_{K}q_{(A,a)}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I_{K}}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,\text{\char 232\relax}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{\char 232\relax}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{(A,a)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\atop\simeq}

and pasting this 2-cell with by pasting the following 2-cell with
\theta_{(A,a)}
exhibits
x
as a pseudosection of
a
. This exhibits
K
as a pseudoretract of a representable.
∎

Corollary 4.2.6
.

For any small 2-category
\mathcal{C}
,
{\bf Flat}_{{\bf ps}}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
is finitely bi-accessible.

Proof.

We saw that flat pseudofunctors inherit
\sigma
-filtered and bifiltered bicolimits of
{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
; moreover from
Theorem

4.1.4
we know that any flat pseudofunctor is a
\sigma
-filtered bicolimit of its canonical cocone, so the representable form a generator. Finally, for bicompact objects are retracts of corepresentables, they form an essentially small subcategory for
\mathcal{C}
is small, hence has a small set of pseudo-idempotent.
∎

4.3
Finitely bipresentable 2-categories of flat pseudofunctors

Now we want to refine this result in in the case of a bilex 2-category: here we can replace everywhere the condition of being flat by the condition of being bilex thanks to
Proposition

4.1.8
. We are going to prove that for any small bilex 2-category, the corresponding 2-category of flat (aka bilex) pseudofunctors is finitely bipresentable. The strategy is the following:

4.3.2

we prove that the category of flat pseudofunctors is bicomplete - in fact, its bilimits are computed in the 2-category of pseudofunctors,

4.3.4

then we prove that bilex 2-categories have bisplitting of pseudoidempotent,

4.3.5

then we deduce that bicompact are exactly the birepresentable in the bilex context,

4.3.6

then combining those result with admissibility ensures the desired result.

We need however the following general observation before anything:

Lemma 4.3.1
.

For a small bilex 2-category
\mathcal{C}
, the Yoneda embedding turns finite bilimits into finite bicolimits.

\mathcal{C}^{{\rm op}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\char 232\relax}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]

Proof.

Let
G:I\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
be with
I
a finite 2-category and
W:I\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
a finite weight. Then for any flat pseudofunctor
F:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
we have an equivalence

\displaystyle{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]\big[\text{\char 232\relax}_{{\textup{bilim}}^{W}G},F\big]

\displaystyle\simeq F({\textup{bilim}}^{W}G)

\displaystyle\simeq\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bilim}}}^{W}F(G(i))

\displaystyle\simeq\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bilim}}}^{W}{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]\big[\text{\char 232\relax}_{G(i)},F\big]

\displaystyle\simeq{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]\big[\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}^{W^{{\rm op}}}\!\text{\char 232\relax}_{G(i)},F\big]

∎

Proposition 4.3.2
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a small bilex 2-category, then the 2-category
{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
has small bilimits, which are created by the pseudofully faithful inclusion

{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{C}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]

Moreover, finite bilimits in
{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
commute with bifiltered bicolimits.

Proof.

Bilimits exist and are pointwise in
{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
: for any
F:I\rightarrow{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
, any weight
W:I\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
and any
C
in
\mathcal{C}
we have

({\textup{bilim}}^{W}\,F)(C)\simeq\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bilim}}}^{W}\,F_{i}(C)

Let us prove that this pseudofunctor is flat. As
\mathcal{C}
is supposed to be bilex, this amounts to check that
{\textup{bilim}}^{W}\,F
is itself bilex. But this is a consequence of commutations of bilimits. Indeed, let
G:J\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
be with
J
a finite and
V:J\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
a finite weight, so that
{\textup{bilim}}^{V}G
exists in
\mathcal{C}
. Then we have

\displaystyle({\textup{bilim}}^{W}F)({\textup{bilim}}^{V}G)

\displaystyle\simeq{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}][\text{\char 232\relax}_{{\textup{bilim}}^{V}G},{\textup{bilim}}^{W}F]

\displaystyle\simeq{\bf ps}[I,{\bf Cat}]\big[W,{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}][\text{\char 232\relax}_{{\textup{bilim}}^{V}G},F(-)]\big]

But observe that in each
i\in I
we have an equivalence

\displaystyle{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}][\text{\char 232\relax}_{{\textup{bilim}}^{V}G},F(i)]

\displaystyle\simeq F(i)({\textup{bilim}}^{V}G)

\displaystyle\simeq\underset{j\in J}{{\textup{bilim}}}^{\!V}F(i)(G(j))

and this provides us with a pseudonatural equivalence

{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}][\text{\char 232\relax}_{{\textup{bilim}}^{V}G},F(-)]\simeq\underset{j\in J}{{\textup{bilim}}}^{\!V}F(-)(G(j))

Now, for we can extract a bilimit on the right of a homcategory, this gives the following chain of equivalences

\displaystyle({\textup{bilim}}^{W}F)({\textup{bilim}}^{V}G)

\displaystyle\simeq{\bf ps}[I,{\bf Cat}]\big[W,\underset{j\in J}{{\textup{bilim}}}^{\!V}F(-)(G(j))]

\displaystyle\simeq\underset{j\in J}{{\textup{bilim}}}^{\!V}[I,{\bf Cat}]\big[W,{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}][\text{\char 232\relax}_{G(j)},F(-)]\big]

\displaystyle\simeq\underset{j\in J}{{\textup{bilim}}}^{\!V}{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]\big[\text{\char 232\relax}_{G(j)},{\textup{bilim}}^{W}F\big]

\displaystyle\simeq\underset{j\in J}{{\textup{bilim}}}^{\!V}({\textup{bilim}}^{W}F)(G(j))

Hence the pseudofunctor
({\textup{bilim}}^{W}F)
is bilex, hence is in
{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
. Now for we have a pseudo-fully faithful inclusion, we know that this is already a bilimit there.
∎

In the general case, we saw that bicorepresentables were bicompact, but one had also to consider pseudoretracts of bicorepresentables to have all the bicompacts. In the bilex case, we prove that this simplifies, as the one dimensional case. To this end we introduce the following 2-dimensional analog of idempotents:

Definition 4.3.3
.

In a 2-category
\mathcal{C}
, a
pseudoidempotent
is a 1-cell
e:A\rightarrow A
equipped with an invertible 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon\atop\simeq}

Now a
bisplitting
of a pseudoidempotent is a pair of invertible 2-cell as below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta\atop\simeq}

Lemma 4.3.4
.

A bilex 2-category is closed under splitting of pseudoidempotents.

Proof.

Consider the following bi-iso-inserter

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\textup{biIsoIns}}(e,1_{A})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{(e,1_{A})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{(e,1_{A})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{(e,1_{A})}\atop\simeq}

Then observe that precomposing the parallel pair
(e,1_{A})
with
e
also insert an invertible 2-cell
\upsilon
so the universal property of the bilimit provides us with a canonical map
r
and a pair of invertible 2-cells
\alpha,\beta
as below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\textup{biIsoIns}}(e,1_{A})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{(e,1_{A})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{(e,1_{A})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{(e,1_{A})}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta\atop\simeq}

which form altogether with the composite
i_{(e,1_{A})}i_{(\mu_{(e,1_{A})}}
a biplitting of the pseudoidempotent
e
.
∎

Theorem 4.3.5
.

For any bilex 2-category
\mathcal{C}
, we have a biequivalence

\mathcal{C}^{{\rm op}}\simeq({\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}])_{\omega}

In other words, bicompact objects are bicorepresentables and bicorepresentables are bicompact in
{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
.

Proof.

At this point the theorem appears as a corollary of the previous lemmas. We saw that birepresentable are bicompact; conversely we saw that any bicompact object is a pseudoretract of a representable

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\text{\char 232\relax}_{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\atop\simeq}

But then we have a pseudo-idempotent in
{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\text{\char 232\relax}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\text{\char 232\relax}_{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

which comes uniquely by full faithfulness of
from a pseudo-idempotent
e:A\rightarrow A
in
\mathcal{C}
. But now as
\mathcal{C}
is bilex, this pseudo-idempotent has a bisplitting in
\mathcal{C}
,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\prime}\atop\simeq}

which is preserved by
. Now by uniqueness up to equivalence of bisplitting, we must have a pseudonatural equivalence
K\simeq\text{\char 232\relax}_{B}
.
∎

Theorem 4.3.6
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small bilex 2-category. Then
{\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
is finitely bipresentable, and
\mathcal{C}
can be chosen as a generator of bicompact objects.

5
2
-dimensional Gabriel-Ulmer duality

5.1
Bi-accessible pseudofunctors

Definition 5.1.1

(Bi-accessible pseudofunctors)
.

A pseudo-functor between finitely bi-accessible
2
-categories is finitely bi-accesssible if it preserves bifiltered bicolimits. Similarly we say that it is
\lambda
-bi-accessible if it preserves
\lambda
-bifiltered bicolimits.

Proposition 5.1.2
.

Let
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}
be finitely bi-accessible categories. There is a
1
-to-
1
correspondence between finitely bi-accessible pseudofunctors
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
and pseudofunctors
\mathcal{A}_{\omega}\to B
, which is induced by biKan extension.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textup{biLan}}_{i}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Proof.

Several things need to be shown. We start from showing that given a pseudofunctor
f:\mathcal{A}_{\omega}\to\mathcal{B}
, its left biKan extension preserves bifiltered bicolimits. This follows from the fact that we have an explicit way to compute the biKan extension. First observe that, combining the cancellation rule observed at
1.3.3
together with
Proposition

1.3.6
knowning that here both
\iota
and
\nu_{\mathcal{A}}
are pseudofully-faithful, all the 2-cells in the following diagram happen to be natural equivalences:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\bf ps}[(\mathcal{A}_{\omega})^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textup{biLan}}_{i}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textup{biLan}}_{\text{\char 136\relax}}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Now, both binerve pseudofunctors
\nu_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\nu_{\mathcal{B}}
preserve bifiltered bicolimits by
Proposition

4.2.2
and
Theorem

4.1.9
, the biKan extension
{\textup{biLan}}_{\text{\char 136\relax}}\nu_{\mathcal{B}}f
preserves all bicolimits by its universal property. Thus, the composition
{\textup{biLan}}_{\text{\char 136\relax}}(\nu_{\mathcal{B}}f)\nu_{\mathcal{A}}
preserves bifiltered bicolimits. Moreover, it lands by construction in the closure under bifiltered bicolimits of the corepresentables, and thus lifts against
\nu_{\mathcal{B}}
, and this lift is exhibited as being the biKan extension
{\textup{biLan}}_{\iota}f

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\bf ps}[(\mathcal{A}_{\omega})^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\bf ps}[(\mathcal{B}_{\omega})^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textup{biLan}}_{\iota}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{\mathcal{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textup{biLan}}_{\text{\char 136\relax}}\nu_{\mathcal{B}}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

∎

Theorem 5.1.3
.

Let
F:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
be a
\lambda
-biaccessible pseudofunctor between
\lambda
-bipresentable
2
-categories. Then in the diagram below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\iota}

F
coincides up to a natural equivalence with the with the biKan extension
\text{biLan}_{\iota}F\iota
.

Proof.

It follows from the density of
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
, the fact that biKan extensions are pointwise.
∎

5.2
Adjoint Functor Theorems

In this section we will prove two versions of the adjoint functor theorem. In both cases, we reduce the existence of the left/right adjoint to the computation of a biKan extension, this is possible thanks to a classical result that we state without proof below. While the proof does not explicitely appear in the literature, it is a
2
-dimensional analog of
[
Bor94
, 3.7.2]
and the proof carries to our context without any effort.

Lemma 5.2.1
.

Let
L:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
be a pseudofunctor between
2
-categories. Then
L
has a right adjoint if and only if the following two conditions hold.

(a)

{\textup{biLan}}_{F}(1_{\mathcal{A}})
exists,

(b)

F
preserves it.

Of course, the dual version holds for left adjoints and right biKan extensions.

Theorem 5.2.2
.

Let
L:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
be a pseudofunctor preserving all weighted bicolimits between finitely bipresentable
2
-categories. Then it has a right biadjoint.

Proof.

We reduced to show that:

(a)

{\textup{biLan}}_{F}(1_{\mathcal{A}})
exists,

(b)

F
preserves it.

Indeed, in this case
{\textup{biLan}}_{F}(1_{\mathcal{A}})
provides a right biadjoint for
F
. (a) Because
\mathcal{A}
is a large
2
-category, we cannot apply on the spot any bi-analog of
[
Bor94
, 3.7.2]
, thus we need to massage the biKan extension. Consider the following diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textup{biLan}}_{L\iota}\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}

We claim that, if it exists,
{\textup{biLan}}_{L\iota}\iota
is pseudonaturally isomorphic to
{\textup{biLan}}_{L}1_{\mathcal{A}}
by application of the cancellation rule
1.3.3
:

{\textup{biLan}}_{L\iota}\iota\simeq{\textup{biLan}}_{L}{\textup{biLan}}_{\iota}\iota\simeq{\textup{biLan}}_{L}1_{\mathcal{A}}

Now applying
1.3.3
, we know that in the the following diagram the leftmost 2-cell is invertible:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\bf ps}[(\mathcal{A}_{\omega})^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}[L\iota,-]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textup{biLan}}_{\text{\char 136\relax}}\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textup{biLan}}_{L\iota}\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\iota*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

exhibiting
{\textup{biLan}}_{L\iota}\iota
as the composite
{\textup{biLan}}_{\text{\char 136\relax}}\iota\mathcal{B}[L\iota,-]
, which both exist, ensuring it itself exists, and so does the desired
{\textup{biLan}}_{L}1_{\mathcal{A}}
.

(b) Using
Proposition

1.3.2
(which is the results of
[
DDS18
, Sec. 4]
), we know that if
{\textup{biLan}}_{F}1_{\mathcal{A}}
exists, it is computed via a weighted bicolimits, in complete analogy to the
1
-dimensional theory of Kan extensions. Thus, because
F
is bicocontinuous, there is no doubt that it would preserve
{\textup{biLan}}_{F}1_{\mathcal{A}}
.
∎

Theorem 5.2.3
.

Let
R:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
be a pseudofunctor preserving all weighted bilimits and bifiltered colimits between finitely bi-accessible
2
-categories with weighted bilimits. Then it has a left biadjoint.

Proof.

As in the proof of
Theorem

5.2.2
, and again by the bi-analog of
[
Bor94
, 3.7.6]
, we are reduced only to show that the right biKan extension in the diagram below exists.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textup{biRan}}_{R}(1_{\mathcal{A}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}

This time we cannot use the previous proof strategy to finish the proof, because we would need a codense (as opposed to dense) sub-
2
-category of
\mathcal{A}
. So we need to be more subtle. Call
{\bf ps}[\mathcal{A},{\bf Cat}]_{s}\hookrightarrow{\bf ps}[\mathcal{A},{\bf Cat}]
the full
2
-subcategory spanned by those pseudofunctors that are
small
weighted bilimits of corepresentables and consider the following diagrams.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\bf ps}[\mathcal{A},{\bf Cat}]_{s}^{\rm op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{biRan}_{R}1_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{\char 232\relax}^{\rm op}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}[-,R]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{biRan}_{\text{\char 232\relax}^{\rm op}}1_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R*}

The outer biKan extension exists because
\mathcal{A}
has small weighted bilimits (in fact the two conditions are equivalent by the dual version
[
DDS18
, Sec. 4]
). If we prove that
\mathcal{B}[-,R]
is well defined, then we finish as in the previous theorem following
Remark

1.3.4
. Putting everything together, we need to show that for every
B
in
\mathcal{B}
, the functor
\mathcal{B}[B,R]:\mathcal{A}\to{\bf Cat}
is a small weighted bicolimit of corepresentables in
{\bf ps}[\mathcal{A},{\bf Cat}]_{s}
. Now, recall that, by
Corollary

3.4.6
and
Remark

3.4.7
,
B
must be
\lambda
-compact for some
\lambda
. Thus the functor
\mathcal{B}(B,R):\mathcal{A}\to{\bf Cat}
preserves
\lambda
-directed colimits for some
\lambda
, thus the theorem follow by
Proposition

1.3.5
and
Theorem

5.1.3
.
∎

5.3
2-dimensional Gabriel-Ulmer Duality

We shall now present a Gabriel-Ulmer duality between bilex
2
-categories and locally finitely bipresentable
2
-categories. While the technical material that will deliver the duality is already disseminated in the previous sections of the paper (
Theorem

4.1.7
and
Theorem

4.3.5
), we must dedicate some paragraphs to clarify the
3
-dimensional setting in which the duality takes place. To do so, we will need to acknowledge that bilex
2
-categories and locally finitely bipresentable
2
-categories can be both organized in tricategories. As a general reference for the theory of tricategories we refer to
[
JY21
, Chap. 11]
.

Remark 5.3.1
.

Before diving in the relevant definitions for this section, we may recall some delicate points related to the usual problem of coherence in higher dimensional category theory. Most of
[
JY21
, Chap. 11]
is dedicated to show that the collection of bicategories, pseudofunctors, pseudonatural transformations and modifications form a tricategory, and this amounts to quite a long and tedious proof. Of course, they cannot have a more strict structure, say that of a
3
-category, because none of the compositions can be defined on the nose due to the pseudo-ness. Despite restricting our attention to strict
2
-categories, our choice of morphisms puts up in a similar situation to that of
[
JY21
, Chap. 11]
. In
[
Shu12
]
, Shulman observes that the tricategory
2\mbox{-}{\bf Cat}_{{\bf ps}}
of
2
-categories, pseudofunctors, pseudonatural transformations and modifications is
iconic
. Iconic tricategories are more general than Gray categories.

Definition 5.3.2

(The tricategory
\mathbf{biLex}
)
.

The tricategory
\mathbf{biLex}
has objects small
2
-categories with weighed finite bilimits in the sense of
Definition

1.1.5
.
1
-cells are pseudofunctors preserving finite bilimits,
2
-cells are pseudonatural transformations and
3
-cells are modifications. The structure of tricategory is inherited by that of
2\mbox{-}{\bf Cat}_{{\bf ps}}
.

Definition 5.3.3

(The tricategory
\mathbf{biP}_{\omega}
)
.

The tricategory
\mathbf{biP}_{\omega}
has objects finitely bipresentable
2
-categories.
1
-cells are right biadjoints preserving bifiltered bicolimits,
2
-cells are pseudonatural transformations and
3
-cells are modifications. The structure of tricategory is inherited by that of
2\mbox{-}{\bf Cat}_{{\bf ps}}
.

Theorem 5.3.4

(
2
-dimensional Gabriel-Ulmer duality)
.

There is a tri-equivalence of tricategories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{biLex}^{{\rm op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{biP}_{\omega}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Mod}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Th}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

5.3.5
\mathbf{Mod}
.

The trifunctor
\mathbf{Mod}:\mathbf{biLex}^{{\rm op}}\to\mathbf{biP}_{\omega}
can be described by,

\mathbf{Mod}(\mathcal{C})=\textbf{biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}].

To be more precise, this correspondence is well-defined at the level of objects because
\textbf{biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]
is indeed a finitely bipresentable
2
-category by
Theorem

4.3.6
. At the level of morphisms, let
F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a pseudofunctor preserving finite bilimits, then the precomposition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textbf{biLex}[\mathcal{D},{\bf Cat}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textbf{biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{*}}

is clearly well defined, indeed for every lex functor
X:\mathcal{D}\to{\bf Cat}
,
XF
is bilex too, because both
X
and
F
are. Now, looking at the commutative diagram below we notice that,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textbf{biLex}[\mathcal{D},{\bf Cat}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textbf{biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\bf ps}[\mathcal{D},{\bf Cat}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\bf ps}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

-

F^{*}
preserves all weighted bilimits. This is because weighted bilimits are created by
\iota_{\mathcal{D}}
(
Proposition

4.3.2
) and
\iota_{\mathcal{C}}
and the precomposition functor on the bottom of the diagram preserves all weighted bilimits and bicolimits.

-

Similarly,
F^{*}
preserves all bifiltered bicolimits, again by applying
Proposition

4.3.2
.

By our version of the adjoint functor theorem (
Theorem

5.2.3
),
F^{*}
is a right biadjoint preserving bifiltered bicolimits. The action of
\mathbf{Mod}
on
2
and
3
-cells is relatively straightforward and does not require much justification, we describe it for the case of
2
-cells. Consider a pseudonatural transformation as below,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

Then of course we get a pseudonatural transformation between the precompositons functors
F^{*}\Rightarrow G^{*}
.

5.3.6
\mathbf{Th}
.

For the trifunctor
\mathbf{Th}:\mathbf{biP}_{\omega}\to\mathbf{biLex}^{{\rm op}}
we proceed as follows,

-

at the level of objects, it maps a finitely bipresentable
2
-category
\mathcal{B}
to the opposite of its full sub
2
-category of bicompact objects
\mathcal{B}\mapsto\mathcal{B}_{\omega}^{{\rm op}}
. Via
Proposition

3.1.12
,
\mathcal{B}_{\omega}
is closed under finite weighted bicolimits and thus
\mathcal{B}_{\omega}^{{\rm op}}
is in
\mathbf{biLex}
.

-

Given a right adjoint preserving bifiltered colimits
R:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
, we know that its left adjoint
L
must map bicompact objects to bicompact objects via
Lemma

3.3.10
. So we define the opposite of its restriction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{B}_{\omega})^{{\rm op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{A}_{\omega})^{{\rm op}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{L^{{\rm op}}}}

to be the image of
R
under the action of
\mathbf{Th}
.

-

For the behavior of
\mathbf{Th}
at the level of
2
-cells and
3
-cells, recall that the
2
-category of right biadjoints, pseudonatural transformations and modifications is biequivalent to the opposite of left biadjoints, pseudonatural transformations and modifications
RAdj(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})\simeq LAdj(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})^{\rm op}
and thus we can easily define
\mathbf{Th}
on
2
and
3
-cells as we did for
\mathbf{Mod}
.

Proof of
Theorem

5.3.4
.

As discussed in
Remark

3.2.12
and in the first lines of
Theorem

4.1.7
, we have a pseudofunctor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\bf Flat}_{\bf ps}[(\mathcal{B}_{\omega})^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{\mathcal{B}}}

which is easy to acknowledge as a morphism of locally finitely bipresentable
2
-categories by
Lemma

3.2.13
. The collection of all the
\nu
’s gives us a pseudonatural transformation
1\Rightarrow\mathbf{Mod}\circ\mathbf{Th}
.
Theorem

4.1.7
proves that such transformation is a pointwise biequivalence of
2
-categories.
Similarly to the previous discussion, the Yoneda embedding discussed in
Theorem

4.3.5
, gives a biequivalence
\mathcal{C}^{{\rm op}}\simeq({\bf biLex}[\mathcal{C},{\bf Cat}])_{\omega}
in
\mathbf{biLex}
which yields a pseudonatural transformation
\mathbf{Th}\circ\mathbf{Mod}\Rightarrow 1
, which – again – is shown to be a pointwise biequivalence of
2
-categories.
∎

6
Examples

6.1
Cat

The following ur-example is key to the next results. Recall that in
{\bf Cat}
, finite categories are bicompact, which we already proved at
Lemma

3.1.10
.

Theorem 6.1.1
.

{\bf Cat}
is finitely bipresentable.

Proof.

1 and 2 are strong generators, and they are bicompacts: hence
{\bf Cat}
admits a strong generator of bicompact objects, which ensures it to be finitely bipresentable from
Theorem

3.4.3
.
∎

6.2
2-categories of pseudo-algebras of bifinitary pseudomonads

There is a well known theory of pseudomonads and their algebras (see for example
[
LMV02
]
), for which we dispense us of definition.

Definition 6.2.1
.

A pseudomonad is said to be
bifinitary
if it preserves bifiltered bicolimits.

It is well known since
[
BKP89
]
[Theorem 5.8] that 2-categories of
strict
algebras and pseudomorphisms for finitary 2-monads are bicocomplete. However, for our purposes, in particular in the context of
\Phi
-exactness we are investigating in the last subsection, we need a corresponding statement concerning the 2-category of
pseudo-algebras
, furthermore in the case of a
pseudomonad
. This is the content of
[
Osm24
]
, from which we use the following result:

Proposition 6.2.2
.

[
Osm24
]
[Theorem 4.19]
Let
(T,\eta,\mu,(\xi,\zeta,\rho))
be a bifinitary pseudomonad on a bicomplete and bicocomplete 2-category
\mathcal{C}
. Then the 2-category of pseudo-algebras and pseudomorphisms
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
is bicocomplete.

The theorem below is the 2-categorical analog of the famous result of
[
GU06
]
:

Theorem 6.2.3
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a finitely bipresentable 2-category and
T
a bifinitary pseudomonad on
\mathcal{B}
. Then
T\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}
is also finitely bipresentable, and the forgetful 2-functor
U_{T}:T\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
is finitely bi-accessible.

Proof.

From
proposition

6.2.2
, we know that
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
is bicocomplete for
T
is bifinitary and
\mathcal{B}
is bicomplete (see
corollary

3.3.7
) and bicocomplete as a finitely bipresentable 2-category. Though arbitrary bicolimits in
T\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}
need not be preserved by the forgetful functor, we are going to prove that bifiltered bicolimits are. We claim that they are computed as follows: for
F:I\rightarrow T\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}
a 2-functor with
I
bifiltered, with
F(i)=(A_{i},a_{i})
, we have

T(\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,A_{i})\simeq\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,TA_{i}

which, together with the morphism induced by the universal property of the bicolimit at the composites
q_{i}a_{i}
, provides us with a structure of pseudomorphism of pseudo-algebras for the bicolimit inclusions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tq_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle q_{i}a_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

so the structure of
T
-pseudo-algebra is induced as the universal map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TAi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle q_{i}a_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\alpha_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{i}\atop\simeq}

The top and left squares exhibit the bicolimit inclusions as pseudomorphisms of
T
-algebras. Hence
T\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}
has bifiltered bicolimits. We left the verification that those data satisfy the coherence condition of pseudoalgebras and pseudomorphisms to the careful reader.

We must prove that the free algebras on bicompacts form a strong generator of bicompact objects for
T\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}
. Consider the 2-adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

From
Lemma

3.3.10
, we know that free algebras on bicompacts are bicompact as the free algebra functor is left 2-adjoint to the forgetful functor which preserves bifiltered pseudocolimits. Moreover, as bicompact objects form a dense generator, the 2-functors
\mathcal{B}[K,-]
jointly reflects equivalences, as well as their restriction to objects that bear a structure of algebra; but by 2-adjunction we have at each
(A,a)
of
T\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}
natural isomorphisms of categories
\mathcal{B}[K,U_{T}(A,a)]\simeq T\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}[F_{T}(K),(A,a)]
, which provides a natural equivalence of functors

\mathcal{B}[K,U_{T}]\simeq T\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}[F_{T}(K),-]

Hence the representable
T\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}[F_{T}(K),-]
jointly reflect equivalences in
T\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}
: hence they form a strong generator.
Theorem

3.4.3
then ensures that
T\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}
is finitely bipresentable.
∎

6.3
{\bf Lex}

Recall that
{\bf Lex}
is the 2-category of small lex categories and lex functors - where lex functors preserve finite limits only up to isomorphism. We will prove that
{\bf Lex}
is finitely bipresentable. It is well known (see for instance
[
BKP89
]
) that
{\bf Lex}
is the 2-category of pseudo-algebras and pseudomorphisms for a finitary KZ-monad on
{\bf Cat}
. For the sake of completeness, we will prove again the finitary part of the result, as the rank of accessibility of
{\bf Lex}
is really crucial here.

6.3.1
.

Let us give a few words on why
{\bf Lex}
is KZ-monadic on
{\bf Cat}
. Consider the free completion under finite limits

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf Cat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf Cat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf Lex}[\mbox{-}]}

sending a small category to its free completion under finite limits - which is still small; this functor defines a pseudomonad on
{\bf Cat}
. This monad is well known to be KZ, and we have a biequivalence exhibiting
{\bf Lex}
as the 2-category of pseudo-algebras for this KZ-monad

{\bf Lex}\simeq{\bf Lex}[-]\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}

Hence
{\bf Lex}
is ensured to have bicolimits as well as pseudolimits - and then bilimits; the later are also preserved by the pseudo-faithful 2-functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf Lex}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf Cat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}

Beware that this functor is not full, which prevents us to use directly reflection theorems bipresentability as they uses fullness.

Lemma 6.3.2
.

{\bf Lex}
is closed in
{\bf Cat}
under bifiltered bicolimits.

Proof.

Recall that one can always use a pseudocolimit as a bicolimit; but in
{\bf Cat}
, pseudocolimit are obtained as localization of oplaxcolimits. Let
\mathcal{C}_{(-)}:I\rightarrow{\bf Lex}
be a bifiltered diagram of lex categories and lex functors. Then the can consider the Grothendieck construction (which is an opfibration on
I
)
{\textup{oplaxcolim}}_{i\in I}\mathcal{C}_{i}
, and obtain the pseudocolimit in
{\bf Cat}
as (the underlying category of) its localization at opcartesian morphisms

\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{pscolim}}}\;\mathcal{C}_{i}\simeq\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;\mathcal{C}_{i}[\textbf{opCart}^{-1}]

We claim that
{\textup{pscolim}}_{i\in I}\;\mathcal{C}_{i}
already is lex. It is clear it is still small as
I
and each
\mathcal{C}_{i}
are. Moreover, its finite limits are computed as follows. For each finite diagram
G:J\rightarrow{\textup{pscolim}}_{i\in I}\;\mathcal{C}_{i}
with
J
a finite category, we can pick for each
j
a representing object
(i_{j},c_{j})
for
G(j)
; then for
J
is finite, there is by
Proposition

2.1.6
a pseudocone
(d_{j}:i_{j}\rightarrow i_{J})_{j\in J}
in
I
, which produces then a diagram
(f_{d_{j}}(c_{j}))_{j\in J}
in
\mathcal{C}_{i_{J}}
, which admits hence a limit in
\mathcal{C}_{i_{J}}
. Then
(i_{J},\lim_{j\in J}f_{d_{j}}(c_{j}))
is a representant for a limit in
{\textup{pscolim}}_{i\in I}\;\mathcal{C}_{i}
.

If now one has a pseudocone
(f_{i}:\mathcal{C}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{C})_{i\in I}
in
{\bf Lex}
, then we have in particular a two-steps strict factorization in
{\bf Cat}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;\mathcal{C}_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{pscolim}}}\,\mathcal{C}_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle f_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\langle f_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}}}

Hence the induced functor
\overline{\langle f_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}}
is lex as one has for any finite diagram
G:J\rightarrow{\textup{pscolim}}_{i\in I}\mathcal{C}_{i}

\displaystyle\overline{\langle f_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}}([(i_{J},\lim_{j\in J}\,f_{d_{j}}(c_{j}))]_{\sim_{I}})

\displaystyle\simeq\langle f_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}(i_{J},\lim_{j\in J}\,f_{d_{j}}(c_{j}))

\displaystyle\simeq f_{i_{J}}(\lim_{j\in J}\,f_{d_{j}}(c_{j}))

\displaystyle\simeq\lim_{j\in J}\,f_{i_{J}}f_{d_{j}}(c_{j})

\displaystyle\simeq\lim_{j\in J}\,f_{i_{j}}(c_{j})

\displaystyle\simeq\lim_{j\in J}\,\overline{\langle f_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}}([(i_{j},c_{j})]_{\sim_{I}})

Hence
{\textup{pscolim}}_{i\in I}\;\mathcal{C}_{i}
, though computed in
{\bf Cat}
, is a small lex category and provides a bicolimit in
{\bf Lex}
. Hence
{\bf Lex}
is closed in
{\bf Cat}
under bifiltered bicolimits.
∎

Theorem 6.3.3
.

{\bf Lex}
is finitely bipresentable.

Proof.

From
Lemma

3.3.10
we know that free lex categories on finite categories are bicompact in
{\bf Lex}
. Now
{\bf Lex}
is the category of strict algebras and pseudomorphisms of the
{\bf Lex}[-]
-monad on
{\bf Cat}
, which is finitary by
Lemma

6.3.2
. Hence by
Theorem

6.2.3
, it is finitely bipresentable and its forgetful functor is finitely accessible.
∎

Also, we end here with a short lemma to ensure that as expected finitely generated lex categories are bicompact:

Lemma 6.3.4
.

A finitely generated lex category – that is, the free lex category over a finite category – is bicompact in
{\bf Lex}

Proof.

Trivial from the bicompactness of finite categories in
{\bf Cat}
, using the universal property of the free completion under finite limits.
∎

Remark 6.3.5
.

It was expected that
{\bf Lex}
should be finitely bipresentable. Morally, this is because
{\bf Lex}
should be thought of as a 2-category of models of some 2-limit theory with all the finite diagrams as arities. We should give a remark here about 2-dimensional limits and colimits in
{\bf Lex}
. It is known that
{\bf Lex}
inherit bilimits from
{\bf Cat}
as a category of pseudo-algebras, and bilimits of
{\bf Cat}
being actually pseudolimits, so are they in
{\bf Lex}
. Moreover,
[
Bou20
]
tells us they are actually
flexible
. However, it has not all strict 2-limits, see
[
BG88
, 7.3]
. Moreover, it only has bicolimits (some of them behave in a surprizing way, being also pseudolimits of some diagrams, see
[
Col16
]
) though bifiltered ones are pseudocolimits as being computed in
{\bf Cat}
). However, having only bicolimits rather than pseudo-ones or strict ones,
[
Kel82
]
does not apply for it requires enriched colimits. Concerning
[
Bou20
]
, we believe that it does not totally cover the 2-dimensional structure involved in bipresentability for it only considers 1-dimensional colimits.

6.3.6
Monoidal categories.

Another example is provided by the 2-category
{\bf Mon}_{\textup{s}}
of
monoidal categories
2
2

2

Notice that by monoidal category we mean a monoidal structure whose coherences are only up-to-isomorphism.
and
strong monoidal functors
. As explained in
[
Lac09
]
[4.1], one can construct a 2-monad on
{\bf Cat}
whose underlying functor maps

A\mapsto\coprod_{n\in\mathbb{N}}A^{n},

where
A^{n}
is the
n^{th}
power of
A
: this is the free monoidal category on the category
A
. Then one can show that monoidal categories are the pseudo-algebras of this 2-monad, while strong monoidal functors are the pseudomorphisms. Similarly,
lax monoidal functors
correspond to the
lax morphisms of algebras
.

Proposition 6.3.7
.

The 2-category
{\bf Mon}_{\textup{s}}
is locally finitely bipresentable.

Proof.

Following
Theorem

6.2.3
we have to prove the 2-monad above to be finitary. But this comes from the fact that
{\bf Cat}
is itself locally finitely bipresentable, so that finitely weighted bilimits commute with bifiltered bicolimits here; but for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
the power with
n
is a finite weighted bilimit, and combined with the commutation of bicolimits with coproducts, this ensure the 2-monad for free monoidal category to be finitary.
∎

Remark 6.3.8
.

One could also ask whether the category
{\bf Mon}_{\textup{lax}}
of monoidal categories and lax monoidal functors is locally finitely bipresentable: but we know this cannot be the case, for this 2-category lacks bicolimits. This could be expected for our
Theorem

6.2.3
does not apply to 2-categories of pseudo-algebra with lax morphisms. However, we do not know whether it is biaccessible or not.

6.4
Reg, Ex, Coh, Ext, Adh, Pretop

Here we capture a large class of examples thanks to
[
GL12
]
amongst the different flavours of exact categories: regular, exact, extensive, coherent categories and (finitary) pretopoi. Those were unified under the formalism of
\Phi
-exactness
, which we will recall briefly before proving that the
\Phi
-exact categories it studies are instances of finitely bipresentable 2-categories.

6.4.1
\Phi
-exactness à la Garner and Lack.

In the following,
\Phi
denotes a class of weights
W:I^{{\rm op}}\rightarrow{\bf Set}
, where each
I
is finitely complete. We will have to suppose them to be finite in the sense of
Definition

1.1.5
. For such a
\Phi
and a category
\mathcal{C}
, we can consider the category
\Phi_{l}(\mathcal{C})
as the full subcategory of the presheaf category
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
consisting is the closure of the representables in
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
under finite limits and
\Phi
-lex-colimits (see
[
GL12
, Sec. 3, especially 3.1]
). A small category
\mathcal{C}
is
\Phi
-lex-cocomplete
if it is lex, and for any weight
W:I^{{\rm op}}\rightarrow{\bf Set}
in
\Phi
and any
lex
functor
F:I\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
in
{\bf Lex}
, the colimit
{\textup{colim}}^{W}_{I}\,F
exists already in
\mathcal{C}
– beware that the functor we compute the colimit of has to be lex, as the indexing category. This amounts to requiring the existence of a left adjoint

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi_{l}(\mathcal{C})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Now a
\Phi
-lex-cocomplete category is said to be
\Phi
-exact
if this left adjoint is lex, which amounts to saying that
(\mathcal{C},L_{\mathcal{C}})
bears a structure of pseudo-algebra for the pseudomonad
\Phi_{l}
on
{\bf Lex}
. Our proof technique will be based on an analysis of the forgetful functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi_{l}\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf Lex}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{\Phi}}

Of course, by construction, the underlying category of
U_{\Phi}\Phi_{l}(\mathcal{C})
has as objects pairs
(W,F)
with
W:I^{{\rm op}}\rightarrow{\bf Set}
a weight in
\Phi
and
F:I\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
a functor, this observation will be relevant in the next Lemma.

We are going to prove that the 2-categories of pseudo-algebras and pseudomorphisms for the pseudomonad
\Phi_{l}
on
{\bf Lex}
are finitely bipresentable if
\Phi
consists of weight indexed by finitely generated lex categories: this will be done by showing the forgetfull functor
U_{\Phi}:\Phi_{l}\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}\rightarrow{\bf Lex}
to be finitary. We need first the following lemma ensuring that
U_{\Phi}
preserves bifiltered colimits of free pseudo-algebras:

Lemma 6.4.2
.

Suppose that
\Phi
consists of weights indexed by finitely generated lex categories and
I
is bifiltered; then for any 2-functor
F:I\rightarrow{\bf Lex}
, one has

U_{\Phi}\Phi_{l}(\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,F(i))\simeq\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,U_{\Phi}\Phi_{l}F(i)

Proof.

Take a weight
W:J\rightarrow{\bf Set}
in
\Phi
and a lex functor
D:J\rightarrow{{\textup{bicolim}}}_{i\in I}\,F(i)
: this is an object of the underlying category of
U_{\Phi}\Phi_{l}({\textup{bicolim}}_{I}F)
. Now for
J
is finitely generated, it is bicompact in
{\bf Lex}
as observed in
Lemma

6.3.4
so the lex functor
D
factorizes through some
F(i)
in
{\bf Lex}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(i)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{\prime}}

But this latter factorization
D^{\prime}
defines an object of
\Phi_{l}F(i)
. It is routine to check such lifts are functorial and induce the desired equivalence.
∎

Lemma 6.4.3

(Gluing pointswise adjunctions into global adjunctions)
.

Let
I
be a small 2-category and
F,G:I\rightrightarrows\mathcal{C}
parallel 2-functors admitting both a bicolimit in
\mathcal{C}
, together with a pair of pseudonatural transformations
R:F\Rightarrow G
and
L:G\Rightarrow F
forming an adjunction
L\dashv R
in
[I,\mathcal{C}]_{p}
. Then the induced functors
\langle q^{\prime}_{i}R_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}
and
\langle q_{i}L_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}
between the respective colimits form an adjunction.

Proof.

Let
q:F\Rightarrow\Delta_{{\textup{bicolim}}_{I}F}
be and
q^{\prime}:G\Rightarrow\Delta_{{\textup{bicolim}}_{I}G}
the corresponding bicolimiting cocones; the natural unit
1_{G}\Rightarrow RL
and counit
LR\Rightarrow 1_{F}
induce the following natural modifications in
[I,\mathcal{C}]

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{{\textup{bicolim}}_{I}G}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}RL}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}*\eta}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{{\textup{bicolim}}_{I}F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces qLR}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q*\epsilon}

But now functoriality of the universal property of the bicolimits defines two 2-cells in
\mathcal{C}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{1_{{\textup{bicolim}}_{I}G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\langle q^{\prime}_{i}R_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}\langle q_{i}L_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle q^{\prime}*\eta\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\langle q_{i}L_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}\langle q^{\prime}_{i}R_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{1_{{\textup{bicolim}}_{I}G}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle q*\epsilon\rangle}

We must prove those two 2-cells to satisfy the triangles identities of adjunctions: but this is just a consequence of the functoriality of the equivalences between homcategories, combined to the fact that
\eta
,
\epsilon
satisfy already those identities.
∎

Lemma 6.4.4

(Bifiltered bicolimits of
\Phi
-exact categories are computed in Cat)
.

For
\Phi
a class of finitely generated weights,
U_{\Phi}
is bifinitary.

Proof.

If
I
is bifiltered and
F:I\rightarrow\Phi_{l}\mbox{-}{\bf Alg}
is a 2-functor, then for each
i
in
I
we have an adjunction with
L_{i}:\Phi_{l}F(i)\rightarrow F(i)
lex:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(i)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{\Phi}\Phi_{l}F(i)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Then we can compose each left adjoint
L_{i}
with the corresponding colimit inclusion
q_{i}:F(i)\rightarrow{{\textup{bicolim}}}_{i\in I}\,F(i)
to get a cocone in
{\bf Cat}
as below, which lifts to a functor
\langle q_{i}L_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}
as below from
Lemma

6.4.2
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,F(i)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{\Phi}\Phi_{l}(\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,F(i))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{i\in I}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\,U_{\Phi}\Phi_{l}F(i)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(i)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{\Phi}\Phi_{l}F(i)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle q_{i}L_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}}

Moreover we know this functor
\langle q_{i}L_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}
to be lex for the bifiltered colimits above are created by the forgetful functor
{\bf Lex}\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
. We must prove that this functor is left adjoint to the unit of
{{\textup{bicolim}}}_{i\in I}\,F(i)
. The unit
\eta:1_{\bf Lex}\Rightarrow\Phi_{l}
is natural, while at each
d:i\rightarrow j
we took a pseudomorphism of algebras
(F(d),\alpha_{d})
as below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi_{l}F(i)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi_{l}F(j)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(i)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(j)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{l}F(d)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(d)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{d}\atop\simeq}

so that the data of all
L_{i}
defines a pseudonatural transformation
U_{\Phi}\Phi_{l}\Rightarrow F
we denote as
L
. Then, from
Lemma

6.4.3
, we know that the induced
\langle q_{i}L_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}
and
\langle q_{i}^{\prime}\iota_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}
form an adjunction in
{\bf Cat}
. This achieves to prove that the filtered bicolimit is canonically equiped with a structure of pseudo-algebra which is sent to the underlying bifiltered colimit of categories by
U_{\Phi}
, which is hence finitary.
∎

Theorem 6.4.5

(2-categories of
\Phi
-exact categories are finitely bipresentable)
.

If
\Phi
is a class of finitely generated weights, then
\Phi_{l}\mbox{-}{\bf ps}{\bf Alg}
is finitely bipresentable, and moreover its bifiltered colimits are computed in
{\bf Cat}
.

Proof.

Under those assumptions, the pseudomonad
\Phi_{l}
is finitary by
Lemma

6.4.4
, as a composite of
U_{\Phi}\Phi_{l}
which both preserve bifiltered bicolimits. Hence by
Theorem

6.2.3
we know
\Phi_{l}\mbox{-}{\bf Alg}
to be finitely bipresentable.
∎

Corollary 6.4.6
.

The following 2-categories are finitely bipresentable:

-

Reg
, the 2-category of small regular categories and regular functors;

-

Ex
, the 2-category of small (Barr)-exact categories and exact functors;

-

Coh
, the 2-category of small coherent categories and coherent functors;

-

\textbf{Ext}_{\omega}
, the 2-category of small finitely-extensive categories and functors preserving finite coproducts;

-

Adh
, the 2-category of small adhesive categories and adhesive functors;

-

\textbf{Pretop}_{\omega}
, the 2-category of small finitary pretopoi and coherent functors.

Proof.

By
[
GL12
, Sec. 5]
these are all in the hypotheses of the theorem above, as in each case, the weights are indexed by the free lex category over some finite category. Indeed,
Reg
is
[
GL12
, Sec. 5.1]
,
Ex
is
[
GL12
, Sec. 5.2]
,
Coh
is
[
GL12
, Sec. 5.6]
,
\textbf{Ext}_{\omega}
is
[
GL12
, Sec. 5.3]
,
Adh
is
[
GL12
, Sec. 5.7]
. Pretopoi are just exact and extensive categories.
∎

Remark 6.4.7
.

Notice that for classes of weights that are not indexed by finitely generated lex categories, the 2-category of
\Phi
-exact categories is not expected to be finitely bipresentable: for instance the 2-category of categories with filtered colimits where filtered colimits commutes with finite limits discussed in
[
GL12
, Sec. 5.9]
. This would not be surprising, as those correspond to categories with unbounded operations, in analogy with the fact that
preframes
as defined in
[
J+02
]
[Part C, Remark C1.1.2] are not finitely presentable.
